| 29 | **The Singapore–GISAID instruments** — the **2014 A\*STAR/BII long-term cooperation agreement**, and whatever instrument accompanies the **S$15m 2025–2030 commitment** to the GISAID Data Science Centre | Contract / funding instrument | §4.10A.4. **Existence, funder, sum and period are established from GISAID's own website; no text is public and none was obtained.** Legal form, terms of reference, staff employer and controllership for curated third-country submissions are all unknown. ⚠️ **Documented on the German counterparty's site and in a ministerial speech — and nowhere on A\*STAR's own site** (4,077-URL sitemap, zero GISAID pages) | Not published |
| 30 | **NIDB oversight instruments** — terms of reference, membership, criteria and decision records for the **Public Health Oversight Committee** and the **NIDB oversight committee**; and the transfer terms for the clinical/research collection available "in Singapore **and abroad**" | Governance | §4.10.9. **The NIDB Framework itself is published** (CDA webpage, 31 Mar 2026) and names the whole applicable statutory stack — **but nothing below it is.** Neither committee appears in the e-Gazette index ("biorepository" — 0 hits) | Not published |

📌 **Two items formerly on the unobtainable list are now recovered and should be struck from it:** the **Code of Practice for Data Portability** (published as v2.0, effective February 2026, and v1.0, August 2024) and MOH's **2007 Governance Framework for Human Biomedical Research** together with its companion **IRB Operational Guidelines** (both **withdrawn from MOH's live site** and recovered from the web archive). ⭐ **The juxtaposition on that last pair is worth stating: the Bioethics Advisory Committee's live FAQ still grounds the authority of its recommendations in the 2007 Governance Framework — a document MOH has removed from its own website.**

## 6.7 ⚠️ Do not overstate the opacity

The honest finding is **uneven**, not uniformly closed:

- The **NMRC Data Request Form**, containing a complete data use agreement, is **live and publicly downloadable**.
- The **SmartCMS Subscription Agreement** is public.
- **HCSA Licence Conditions are published**, and the two-step by which they are modified is observable end to end (§4.2.3).
- **HIA s 90(4) is better drafted than HBRA s 40**: it requires a publicity notice and free copies to the addressee class, and s 90(5) gives a code no force until that notice is published. HBRA s 40(3) requires neither.
- The **TRUST DAC Annual Report** publishes named members, criteria, process, timelines, caseload and an annex of approvals — more governance transparency than most comparable bodies anywhere.

**The accurate formulation** is that Singapore publishes its governance narratives and withholds its operative contracts; that the public-law instruments are unpublished by legal classification rather than by oversight; and that a regulated party **cannot ascertain the full content of its obligations from public sources alone** — but can ascertain a good deal more of it than a first pass suggests.

---

# 7. KNOWN GAPS AND OPEN QUESTIONS

Stated honestly. A gap named is more useful to the client than a gap glossed.

## 7.1 Method, coverage and residual risk

**What was done.** Fifty-two research files were produced and read for this bundle, covering statute, subsidiary legislation, Bills, consultations, licence conditions, circulars, codes, professional codes, standards, certification frameworks, contracts, treaties and case law. Load-bearing citations were independently re-verified against gazetted primary text in four separate verification passes.

**Three assurances that can be given affirmatively:**

1. **The gazette record is complete for the period.** The entire Subsidiary Legislation Supplement for 2025 (S 1–898) and 2026 (S 1–564, to 14 August 2026) was retrieved, verified gap-free, and searched in full text; likewise the Acts Supplement and Bills Supplement for both years.
2. **The statute text relied on in this bundle is not stale anywhere.** **All 252 Acts in the Acts Supplement for 2020–2026 (107MB) were downloaded, parsed and full-text searched for every one of the 45 statutes this bundle relies on** — no title guessing, zero fetch failures. Seven true omnibus Acts exist in the window; three touched relied-on statutes; the 2020, 2024 and 2026 omnibus Acts are clean. **A reader can be told that the citations were checked against invisible omnibus amendments, not merely against title searches.**
3. **Every keyword negative rests on a scripted count, not a shell search** — see §1.5A. This matters: a shell `grep` crash during the research would have produced a false negative on Blood Banking Service Regulations reg 20(3).
4. **The Hansard citations are clean.** A regex scan of every findings file returned **zero** citations attaching a column number to a Hansard reference.

⚠️ **One bounded residual gap in that assurance.** The omnibus sweep window was **2020–2026**. Pre-2020 omnibus Acts are outside it. The 2020 Revised Edition would ordinarily absorb pre-2020 amendments, so the residual risk is small — **except that the Statutes (Miscellaneous Amendments) (No. 2) Act 2014 (Act 35 of 2014) was still being commenced in tranches in 2020 and 2022**, so provisions of it brought into force after the Revised Edition would not be captured. **This is a known, bounded gap, not a claim of complete coverage.**

## 7.2 Domains that are genuinely unowned

These are gaps in this bundle, not necessarily gaps in Singapore law. In several cases the raw material has been retrieved and sits unanalysed.

| # | Gap | What exists but has not been worked |
|---|---|---|
| **G1** | **Health data in family and criminal proceedings** — subpoena, discovery and production of medical records in family proceedings; the interaction of the HIA Third Schedule with family and criminal process | HIA Third Schedule items **38–41** (Criminal Procedure Code 2010 ×2, State Courts Act 1970, Supreme Court of Judicature Act 1969), **57–58** (Protection from Harassment Act 2014; Women's Charter 1961 mandatory treatment orders), **59–66** (Adoption of Children Act 2022, Children and Young Persons Act 1993, Destitute Persons (Welfare Homes) Rules, Vulnerable Adults Act 2018, Women's Charter (Protection of Women and Girls) Rules); **HIA s 30(5)(b)**; the Mental Capacity Act point in §4.8.2; and an unresolved Coroners Act / TPA s 7 / VSA s 5 tension. **This is a standard client question and the bundle answers only the privilege and production limbs (§4.19, §4.21)** |
| **G2** | **Deceased persons' health data** | **HIA s 32** is a dedicated section headed "Access restrictions relating to health information about deceased individuals" and **has been analysed by nobody**. "Deceased" appears 8 times in the Act. Adjacent material held: Coroners Act ss 8, 16, 17, 30, 31; Registration of Births and Deaths Act 2021 ss 22–23; HOTA s 9 objection register; MTERA's complete absence of provisions; SMC Handbook C7.8 on post-mortem confidentiality. **Five sources touch pieces; nobody assembled it** |
| **G3** | **Prison health data** | "Prison" appears **49 times** in the HIA. Third Schedule items **42–56** whitelist medical examinations under the Prisons Act 1933 and nine sets of detention regulations. **Whitelisting matters: it is an express carve-out from the s 6 employment and insurance "excluded purpose" bar** |
| **G4** | **Occupational health records as a domain** — and a live protective gap | §4.9.5. Fragments exist (WSHA s 27; Incident Reporting Regulations reg 7(3); the MOM examination regimes; HIA Third Schedule items 13–18). **The identified gap — that a clinic doing a work-permit medical acts under MOM conditions, not under the Infectious Diseases Act, so IDA s 25's criminal confidentiality protection may not attach — has never been analysed.** It affects an identifiable vulnerable population |
| **G5** | **International cyber and cyber-norm commitments** | §4.18.3. The Budapest Convention; the UN Cybercrime Convention; the Counter Ransomware Initiative; ASEAN cyber norms; the ASEAN Framework on Digital Data Governance. ⛔ **Do not report any Singapore position on the UN Convention against Cybercrime** |
| **G6** | **Pathogen genomic data** | ✅ **Closed — now researched at §4.10A.4, §4.10.8 and §4.10.9.** Established: the **2014** A\*STAR/BII cooperation agreement and the **S$15m 2025–2030** commitment to the GISAID Data Science Centre; **zero e-Gazette and zero Hansard hits** for "GISAID" and "PathGen"; **PDPA s 4(1)(c) disapplies the Act** because A\*STAR is a public agency; the governing instrument is a **German-law contract with an asymmetric choice-of-law clause**; and **Singapore is neither Party nor signatory to the Nagoya Protocol**. 🔴 **What remains open, and is genuinely open:** the **agreement text itself** (register §6.6 item 29) — the single largest residual gap; **GISAID's required and optional metadata fields**, deliberately not asserted because the field list is login-gated, so **it is not established which patient-level fields travel with a submission**; whether GISAID accepts **raw reads** (which carry human host sequence) or only assembled sequences; and whether **any Singapore data sits inside a PathGen pilot** |
| **G7** | **Medical tourism and foreign patients** | The hooks are visible and unexplored: ConnectNEHR prioritises Singapore-issued identifiers; the 2025 Moratorium defines "Resident" by citizenship, permanent residence, work pass and residence-day thresholds; the MOM examination regimes; and **the Genetic Information Bill consultation uses "Singapore Residents" undefined**. A short entry answering "does the NEHR and HIA regime reach a foreign patient at a private hospital?" would be disproportionately useful |
| **G8** | **Military health data** | HIA Third Schedule items **22** (Enlistment Act 1970), **27**, **55–56**, plus **21** (Civil Defence Act 1986), **23** and **25–26**. MINDEF and SAF medical records governance, and how **HIA s 5** (application to Government) interacts with it, are untouched |
| **G9** | **Private hospital groups** — the negative is unverified | The likely answer is that there are no group-specific instruments, **but nobody has verified that negative**, and an unverified negative is not usable. Note that Implementation Guide v2.0 **changed Batch 1 from "Private Hospital" to "Acute and Community Hospital"** — a scope change with direct consequences |
| **G10** | **Dental, optometry and pharmacy as *records* regimes** | The professional codes are covered (§4.7) but not the records regimes. ⚠️ **Whether there is a dental equivalent of the TCM and optometry "proper records to be kept" regulations is unresolved.** Retail pharmacy is a live HIA contributor class (Batch 3) and is barely addressed — and an **Outpatient Pharmacy Service** consultation opened 1 August 2026 (§3C item 24) |
| **G11** | **School health records** | "School" appears **0 times** in the HIA. HPB's School Health Service is unowned. **No IDA s 57B "specified recipient" notice has been located**, so whether schools are designated is unknown |
| **G12** | **Veterinary and zoonotic interfaces; wastewater surveillance** | "Veterin" appears **0 times** in the HIA. **Wastewater surveillance has no located legal instrument at all** despite the CDA Framework relying on it. Nobody has checked the Environmental Public Health Act, NEA instruments or the Animals and Birds Act |
| **G13** | **Employer-side health-data handling as a domain** | §4.13.3 |
| **G14** | **Clinical trials outside HSA's remit** | Investigator-initiated non-regulated studies fall between the HSA and HBRA regimes. **ICH E6(R3) Annex 2, from 1 January 2027**, sits on that seam |
| **G15** | **Retention, reconciled** | §4.2.2 — the HCSA Licence Conditions, the general-law duties and PDPA s 25 have never been reconciled, and **HCSA s 27(1)'s "prescribed period" is not prescribed** |
| **G16** | **The HIA Third and Fourth Schedules, transcribed in full** | The Third Schedule has **66 numbered entries** covering **fewer distinct written laws** — the Criminal Procedure Code 2010 appears twice (items 38 and 41) and the Women's Charter 1961 twice (items 58 and 65). ⚠️ **Write "66 numbered entries covering fewer distinct written laws", not "66 laws".** Transcribing both Schedules would unlock G1–G4 and G8 in one pass |
| **G17** | **The regulator-access package in Bill 17/2026** | §3B.1 — set out as read, but no clause-level analysis of the full package was done |
| **G18** | **Contact-tracing data after the repeal** | §4.10.1 — what now governs it, and the pre-repeal text in terms, were never established. *(The parliamentary-explanation question is now answered.)* |
| **G19** | **The retention and disposal of donor records** in blood, cord blood and human tissue banking. *(The blood-donation Healthpoints linkage, formerly part of this gap, is now answered — §4.15.4.)* | ✅ **Partly closed.** The Licence Conditions were enumerated: there is **no blood-banking-specific and no cord-blood-banking-specific Licence Condition in existence** — all 9 blood-banking and 7 cord-blood LCs are cross-service instruments; only human tissue banking has service-specific LCs (6 of 15). 🔴 **But the sharpest gap in that domain is now identified and open:** retention for all three services runs through the generic **Licence Conditions on the Retention Periods of Patient Health Records (v1.0)**, which is drafted entirely around the **patient** and **contains no category for donor records**. So blood-bank donor records (reg 20), cord-blood linkage records (reg 20) and tissue-bank donor-identification records (reg 31) sit under a **statutory duty to keep them confidential and secure with no stated retention period and no stated disposal point** |

## 7.3 Open questions of law and fact

| # | Question | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **HBRA s 13(3) against s 13(4)–(5)** — does an IRB waiver relieve the *custodian* of the s 29 disclosure restriction, or only the *researcher*? | §5.4.2. **The most consequential unresolved point of statutory construction in the domain.** No retrieved source resolves it |
| 2 | **Does the Health Information Act supersede the PDPA, or run concurrently with it?** ss 75(6), 79(5) and 64(4) have been read as making the duties concurrent, with no PDPA disapplication clause — against a 2023 consultation statement that the Act would supersede the PDPA | ⚠️ **That reading was performed on the Bill, not the enacted Act, and the verification against Act 1 of 2026 was assigned and never delivered. Do not advance it as a finding** (§4.11.1) |
| 3 | **What does HCSA s 59(8) say, and does it matter?** | ✅ **Moot.** It never commenced and was deleted with effect from 1 November 2022 (§4.2.1) |
| 4 | **Health Promotion Board Act 2001 — Act 5 of 2001 or Act 8 of 2001?** | ⚠️ **[CITATION UNCONFIRMED]** — two sources conflict and neither was verified against the gazetted Act. Resolve by reading the page-2 running header or the assent line |
| 5 | **HP(MD)R 2010 reg 26(5)** — the definition of "standalone mobile application", on which **S 66/2026** turns for its central term | Unverified. The contents list suggests reg 26 is about something else. **Do not assume; verify** |
| 6 | **The full text of Health Products (Therapeutic Products) Regulations 2016 (S 329/2016) and Health Products (CTGTP) Regulations 2021 (S 104/2021)** | Not retrieved; the obligations in §4.12 rest on HSA's descriptions of its own regulations |
| 7 | **S 172/2024, S 173/2024 and S 64/2023 — titles never retrieved** | The S-numbers, effects and dates come from SSO annotations, not from the instruments (§4.10) |
| 8 | **enTRUST's legal basis** | ⚠️ **Its terms cite no statutory basis. Do not assert one.** And the "Standardised Terms of Sharing", a named instrument that enTRUST's own Terms of Use make binding, could not be located |
| 9 | **The National Public Health Laboratory sample-submission obligation** | §4.10.6 — a compulsory transfer of human specimens with no located statutory basis |
| 10 | **The Singapore Stroke Registry's statutory basis; NRDA s 23 regulations; is NRDA s 12 a dead letter?** | §4.10.5 |
| 11 | **IDA s 57B "specified recipients"** — has any notice been issued? | Determines whether schools, employers and dormitory operators are designated. **None located** |
| 12 | **Whether access-committee decisions are amenable to judicial review** | §5.5. No source addresses it; nothing is asserted either way |
| 13 | **The new IHR Art 4.1 National IHR Authority — has one been designated?** | §4.10A.3. Nothing located; **Art 4.2 bis obliges domestic adjustment** |
| 14 | **Whether the hiSG "SG100K" arm connects to PRECISE-SG100K** | §4.17.4. Not established |
| 15 | **What authorised the 2016 MOH clinic audit in *Ling Chia Tien*** | §4.21.5 — **reasoned from the statute then in force, not cited from the judgment. Carry the caveat** |
| 15A | **The Singapore Red Cross's role in the blood-donation and Healthpoints scheme** | §4.15.4. It is placed inside the scheme by HSA's own page and by the Minister's speech, but appears in **neither the PDPA Statutory Bodies Schedule nor the PSGA Schedules** — so it is a full PDPA "organisation" with full consent obligations, and **its role has no published treatment** |
| 15B | **Whether a PSGA s 4 data sharing direction exists for the blood-donation Healthpoints flow** | §4.15.4, §6.4(a). ⭐ **Structurally unfindable by retrieval** — s 4 carries no publication requirement, so no search can answer it and no nil return is evidence. **A parliamentary question is the only route.** Note the question is *narrower than it looks*: even if a direction exists, **s 6(1) lifts only common-law confidentiality, s 6(2) preserves privilege and contract, and s 11(1) forbids inconsistency with any other written law** — so it could not cure reg 20(3), and could not reach yuu Rewards Club at all |
| 15C | ⭐ **Does a genome sequence form part of a "laboratory test report" under HIA First Schedule item 4?** | §4.10.8, §4.21.6, §4.10A.4. **"Laboratory test report" is undefined** — First Schedule Part 2 defines ten other terms and not that one. **It determines whether the National Public Health Laboratory must push sequence-derived results into the NEHR from 1 September 2027.** Resolvable by regulation under **s 3(1)** or amendment under **s 111**. **Nothing published resolves it** — the HIA Implementation Guide's data dictionary is the place to look |
| 15D | **Does Singapore's pathogen genomic surveillance run under IDA s 59A, or under IDA ss 6–10?** | §4.10.7, §4.10A.4. **Not established in either direction.** Decisive, because **if it runs under ss 6–10 then none of s 59A's safeguards attach at all** — no anonymisation default, no s 59A(6) necessity test, no s 59A(7)(b) re-identification offence |
| 15E | **Is a virus genome "data … about an individual" at all?** | §4.10A.4. The anterior question beneath every identifiability analysis of pathogen data. **Not addressed by the PDPA, by any PDPC guidance, or by any Singapore judgment. Do not assert an answer** |
| 16 | **The MediShield / CareShield wage-deduction provision against HIA s 38(5)** | §4.14.4 — unreconciled by anyone |
| 17 | **The complete set of instruments in the Statutes (Miscellaneous Amendments) Act 2025 re-anchoring** | ✅ Resolved: **six statutes, seven definitions** (HBRA, NRDA, CareShield Life, TPA, VSA, HOTA), all commenced 5 December 2025. And the **PDPA Second Schedule Pt 3 Div 1 para 3(a) deletion is spent-limb tidying, not a narrowing** — the deleted limb referred to institutions licensed under the repealed Private Hospitals and Medical Clinics Act, a class already covered by limb (b). ⚠️ **Do not describe it as narrowing the consent exceptions** |

## 7.4 Material that must not be cited

⛔ **The following are unverified or contradicted by primary text and must not appear as fact in any advice drawn from this bundle:**

| Item | Why |
|---|---|
| **S 66/2026 conditions**: ISO 13485 self-attestation; patient notification; a $20,000 / 12-month penalty; annual attestation | **None is in the Order** (§4.12.1) |
| **"ss 98–103 disapply confidentiality obligations"**; **"ss 99–104 each contain an equivalent subsection (3)"** | **s 101 has no confidentiality override; s 104 has no subsections.** Correct range: ss 98(3), 99(3), 100(3), 102(3), 103(3) |
| **"Sensitive health information"** attached to any HIA provision | The word "sensitive" appears **0 times** in the Act — verified by three independent routes |
| **"The HIA has three Schedules"** | It has **four**; the Fourth is the non-NEHR data-sharing table |
| **"The HIA s 98(4) preserves IDA s 25, TPA s 7 and VSA s 5"** | The Act **names no statute** in s 98(4); "Termination of Pregnancy" and "Voluntary Sterilisation" appear **0 times** in it |
| **"Bill 17/2026 confers powers on nine other Acts"** | The Bill's own First Schedule lists **eleven** |
| **"Sitting of 13 February 2026"** | **There was no sitting on that date.** It is 12 February 2026, vol 96, sitting 17 |
| **"Act 20 of 2016" for the Government Technology Agency Act**; **"Act 5 of 2007" for the National Registry of Diseases Act**; **"Acts Supplement 1/2026" for the Health Information Act** | Correct: **Act 23 of 2016**; **Act 56 of 2007**; **Acts Supplement No. 2 of 2026** |
| **"HCSA s 59(8) remains in force"** | Deleted with effect from 1 November 2022 |
| **"IM8 is unpublished"** | Superseded (§6.5) |
| **"The contact-tracing safeguards were removed silently"** | **They were explained at length by the Minister** on 7 March 2024; what is true is that **no MP spoke to them** |
| **Singapore's position on the UN Convention against Cybercrime** | Rests on an AI-generated search summary |
| **A "Synapxe federated trusted research environment across the three clusters"** | A commercial vendor's blog |
| **"Synapxe leads an effort to mandate HL7 FHIR nationally"** | An industry-survey self-report by an unnamed respondent. **Do not attribute to the Government** |
| **ICD-10-AM as a *mandated* coding standard** | No instrument mandating it was located. Say MOH *uses* it |
| **The $1 million genetic-information financial limit** | Expressly an **illustrative assumption** in the consultation paper, not a proposal |
| **"PRECISE-SG100K opens to Singapore public researchers from 2027"** | A preprint surfaced by a search snippet; uncorroborated |
| **An unnamed SMC case with "six charges and an 18-month suspension"** | Uncorroborated; no Grounds of Decision located |
| **Employment pass or immigration refusal for active tuberculosis or HIV** | Unverifiable. The only citable primary hook is **IDA s 25(1)(i)**, which permits disclosure *to* the Controller of Immigration and says nothing about refusal |
| **"IM8 consists of 27 domains and 53 controls" and "maps to 669 other frameworks"** | A vendor claim, **contradicted by GovTech's own published artefacts** |

## 7.5 Two things that would most improve this bundle

1. **Transcribe the Health Information Act's Third and Fourth Schedules in full.** It costs nothing — the enacted text is already retrieved — and it unlocks the prison, military, occupational-health, court-proceedings and deceased-persons gaps in one pass, as well as fixing the "66 laws" error at source.
2. **Answer the HBRA s 13(3) against s 13(4)–(5) question.** It is the one point of construction on which the whole secondary-use architecture turns, and no source in the project resolves it.

---

*End of BUNDLE-CORE. Prepared as at 15 August 2026. Status statements are as at that date; where an instrument takes effect on 17 August 2026 or later, it is identified as pipeline and not as in force.*
